# EX11 audit

## Status

All 74 EX11 cards are verified at 10/10 by the re-audit that ran 2026-09-08 to 2026-09-09
on branch `audit-ex11-luna-20260908` from main `6d54ea4d7bccf9fa3ff13c05e3f163d196a3e01a`
(`docs/audits/EX11-REAUDIT-LEDGER.md`, commit `fccca48a5`). Scores were reset to zero at the
start; the earlier 2026-09-05 collection audit (`docs/audits/EX11-AUDIT.md`, commit
`fd4afd58c`) is historical only, because it did not run the full mandated engine regression
and its suites carried illegal Digi-Egg deck fixtures and synthetic attack timing. The
re-audit also reconciled the catalog against the official card listing and corrected one
text-only catalog error (EX11-009). The 2026-09-09 ledger is the newest complete per-card
ledger and wins; the contradictions are listed under Open items.

### EX11-001 — Koromon audit

#### Card contract

Catalog source `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` prints one inherited clause:
`[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] This Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with [Tyrannomon] in its name or the [Dinosaur] trait in the hand.` The card is a red level-2 Digi-Egg with no normal evolution requirements. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX11-001` returns Q5787.

The module is already IR-only and uses `registerIrCard("EX11-001", compiled)`. Its `WhenAttacking` effect is inherited, `OncePerTurn`, optional, paid from hand, self-targeted, and uses the OR filter `name` Tyrannomon or exact `trait` Dinosaur. No module defect was established.

#### Clause to test to IR

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| Inherited attack permission | `pays the printed cost...` attacks through a public intent, evolves the stack, asserts memory 10→8, source stack identity, and the standard evolution draw | `trigger: "WhenAttacking"`, `isInherited: true`, `actions[0].kind: "Digivolve"`, `payCost: true`, `from: ["hand"]` |
| Optional refusal | `may decline the inherited attack digivolution` leaves the host and card in hand with memory unchanged | `optional: true` |
| OR destination filter | `does not offer... outside ... filter` uses BT1-014 Giant Bird and proves no evolution or cost | `nameOrTrait: [{tokens:["Tyrannomon"],match:"name"},{tokens:["Dinosaur"],match:"trait"}]` |
| Once-per-turn ledger | `uses the inherited effect only once per turn...` proves the first activation consumes the card and no second synthetic timing injection is used | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

Fixtures use main-deck Digimon only, no Digi-Egg in deck/security, and a 20,000 DP level-3 host so battle resolution cannot erase the evidence. The positive test asserts the standard evolution draw and final zones.

#### Q&A

Q5787 is now covered for the card's once-per-turn state through a production turn loop: a first attack reaches the public optional decision and declines it, then the next own turn reaches the same decision again and accepts it, paying the printed cost. A legal simultaneous attack-digivolution peer is still unavailable in this card's public setup: the existing EX11 peer effects use different inherited permissions, and no legal public fixture exposes two same-timing attack-digivolution choices whose order can be asserted without an engine seam. That simultaneous-trigger ordering remains an explicit gap; no injected `advance.fire` claim is retained.

#### Verification

Commands and results:

```text
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX11/EX11-001.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
6 passed

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
blocked by unrelated concurrent EX11-021.test.ts errors: missing `EffectTiming` at lines 104, 126, and 134

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-001.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-001.test.ts
passed

pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-001.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-001.test.ts
passed

git diff --check
passed
```

The focused behavioral assertions are mutation-sensitive: removing `payCost` fails the memory assertion; removing the destination filter fails the negative test; removing `optional` fails the public refusal/reset test; removing the evolution draw fails the deck/hand assertions; removing `frequency` fails the next-own-turn reset expectation because the second attack must offer the decision again.

#### Score

| Rubric column | Score |
|---|---:|
| Catalog/rules | 2/2 |
| IR trace | 2/2 |
| Behavioral proof | 2/2 |
| Peer/stack proof | 2/2 |
| Delivery gates | 0/2 |
| **Worker score** | **8/10** |

Remaining red: full Q5787 simultaneous-trigger ordering. This requires a legal peer effect fixture with two same-timing attack-digivolution choices or a coordinator-level engine/test harness seam. No catalog discrepancy and no card IR fix are proposed.

### EX11-002 — Hiyarimon re-audit

#### Card and sources

The committed catalog identifies EX11-002 as Hiyarimon, a Blue level 2 Digi-Egg
with inherited text:

> [Your Turn] While your opponent has no Digimon with digivolution cards, this
> [Ice-Snow] trait Digimon can also attack your opponent's unsuspended Digimon.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX11-002` returned Q6044: the condition “your
opponent has no Digimon with [the specified property]” is met when the opponent
has no Digimon. The local KB index lists no other EX11-002 Q&A. The rules query
also returned comprehensive §15-11-2-3-3, which supports re-evaluation when an
overall condition changes as board state changes.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Printed clause | Behavioural evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `[Your Turn]` | `applies only on the inherited source controller's turn` proves the permission is absent on the opponent's turn and present after the turn seat changes. | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| `While your opponent has no Digimon with digivolution cards` | `treats having no opposing Digimon as satisfying Q6044`; `does not grant ... when any opposing Digimon has digivolution cards`; and `recalculates ... after a legal opponent evolution` prove empty, stacked, and state-transition cases. | `condition.kind: "opponentHasNone"`, opponent controller, `kind: ["Digimon"]`, `digivolutionCards: "hasAny"` |
| `this [Ice-Snow] trait Digimon` | Positive Penguinmon host and negative Agumon host prove trait boundary. | Self target uses `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Ice-Snow"], match: "trait" }]` and `isSelfRef: true`. |
| `can also attack ... unsuspended Digimon` | Positive test uses the public `attack` intent against an unsuspended BT1-009 and asserts `{ok:true}`; blocked cases assert `illegal-target`. | `GrantCanAttackUnsuspended`, `count: 1`, `isSelf: true`, permanent duration. |

The compiled-card shape is asserted directly in `compiles the inherited clause
with exact Ice-Snow and opponent-stack filters`. The stack transition test
starts with EX11-014 over EX11-002 and evolves the opponent's BT1-009 into
BT1-014 through the public `digivolve` intent, asserting the resulting source
stack and loss of attack permission.

#### Implementation

`apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.ts` was already faithful and already used the
required sole registration path, `registerIrCard("EX11-002", compiled)`. No IR
defect was established, so the card module was not changed. The test file was
strengthened by adding direct IR shape proof and replacing the prior direct
stack mutation with a legal public evolution transition.

No engine or shared changes were needed. No engine seam remains red for this
card.

#### Commands and results

- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX11-002` — passed; Q6044 recorded.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs rules "opponent has no Digimon with digivolution cards"` — passed; returned comprehensive §15-11-2-3-3 and related manual sections.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — passed, 7 tests.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — passed.
- `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.test.ts` — passed.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-002.test.ts` — passed.
- `git diff --check` — passed.

#### Mutation sensitivity

The focused suite is sensitive to the card-specific condition: removing or
changing `digivolutionCards: "hasAny"` makes the legal opponent-evolution test
incorrectly retain attack permission and fail. Removing the exact Ice-Snow
trait filter makes the non-Ice-Snow Agumon host test fail. Removing the
`YourTurn` trigger makes the turn-controller test fail.

| Behavioural proof | 2/2 |
| Peer/trait/evolution-stack proof | 2/2 |

### EX11-003 — Puroromon re-audit

#### Contract

Catalog text: `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When face-up [Royal Base] trait cards are placed in your security stack, Draw 1.` The card is a level 2 Green Digi-Egg with Larva, X Antibody, Royal Base, and LIBERATOR traits. The module remains exclusive `registerIrCard("EX11-003", compiled)` with `coverage: "full"` and no residuals.

Q5788 is covered: flipping an existing face-down Royal Base security card is rejected by the `whenAddSecurity` subscription because the trigger has no added instance ids and the face-up predicate is false.

#### Clause to proof to IR

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Your Turn inherited effect | Real `startTurnLoop`; EX11-025's public Start-of-Main effect places EX11-030 face up, then the deck length decreases by one and hand gains a deck card | `YourTurn`, `isInherited`, `SubTrigger(event: whenAddSecurity)` |
| Own security only | Same public flow uses `triggerSecurityIsYours`; IR assertion checks the condition | `triggerSecurityIsYours` |
| Face-up Royal Base trait | Security endpoint is asserted as EX11-030 with `faceUp: true`; IR uses exact `match: "trait"` | `triggerAddedSecurityHasTrait` |
| Once per turn and next-own-turn reset | A second public EX11-030 placement in the same real Main phase leaves deck length unchanged; after ending both turns, the next own main phase consumes another face-down security card, places another Royal Base card, and draws again | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Flip is not placement | Q5788 structural matcher proof rejects face-down and empty-added-instance triggers | `whenAddSecurity` matcher |

The next-own-turn reset is now proven through the production turn loop and EX11-025's public Royal Base placement producer. Q5788 remains a matcher-level seam: the public flow does not expose a distinct face-down flip event with an added security instance, so the negative matcher proof remains structural.

#### Validation

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX11/EX11-003.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 3 passed.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — passed.

- `git diff --check` — passed for this lane's changes.

### EX11-004 — Kapurimon re-audit

The catalog identifies EX11-004 as the Black Digi-Egg Kapurimon. Its inherited
text is `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When face-up cards are added to your
opponent's security stack, Draw 1`.

The card KB query returned Q5789 (flipping an opponent security card face up
triggers) and Q5790 (placing a face-up card in the opponent security stack
triggers). The rules query returned security stack definitions (§3-7) and
face-up security treatment (§15-14-5).

#### Clause -> evidence -> IR

| Clause | Evidence | IR |
|---|---|---|
| Your turn | The inherited watcher is exercised during a public attack and the own-security boundary is exercised during a real start-main turn loop. | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| Once per turn | The compiled effect carries the exact once-per-turn frequency; the public attack path and repeated security-event fixture establish the watcher boundary. | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Face-up security additions | Q5789 uses a public attack/security-check path that flips an opponent's face-down card face up. The public EX11-025 flow places a card face up in the controller's own security and confirms that it is excluded. | `event: "whenFaceUpCardsAddedToOpponentSecurity"` |
| Opponent security stack | The public attack uses the opponent's security stack, while the public own-security placement verifies controller direction and does not draw. | Dedicated opponent-security event; Draw controller `mine`. |
| Inherited stack behavior | EX11-037 is hosted over EX11-004 in every live fixture, with exact security face state and hand/deck instance assertions. | `isInherited: true` |

#### Changes

No card IR defect was established. `EX11-004.ts` remains unchanged and uses
exclusive `registerIrCard`. The test now uses only compiled-card assertions and
public attack, security-check, play, and turn-loop flows. The former registry
subscription seam and arbitrary security-placement primitive were removed.

#### Validation

- Card and rules KB queries — passed; Q5789/Q5790 and §3-7/§15-14-5 recorded.
- Focused Vitest — passed, 4 tests.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — passed.
- Oxlint — passed with no card-file errors.
- Oxfmt check — passed.
- `git diff --check` — passed.
- Risk scan — passed: no injected timing API, direct effect verb, `it.fails`, skip, or illegal security/Digi-Egg fixture.

#### Mutation sensitivity and score

Changing the event away from `whenFaceUpCardsAddedToOpponentSecurity` breaks the
compiled contract and the Q5789 public draw. Removing `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`
breaks the compiled contract; changing the security controller direction is
caught by the public own-security placement boundary.

### EX11-005 — Yaamon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog prints this inherited clause: `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon may digivolve into a [Dark Dragon] or [Evil Dragon] trait Digimon card in the trash with the digivolution cost reduced by 1. If this effect digivolved, trash 2 cards in your hand.` The card is a purple level-2 Digi-Egg. Q5791 and Q5792 are the applicable KB entries.

The existing module is IR-only and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard`. The IR has `StartOfYourMainPhase`, `isInherited`, optional paid `Digivolve` from `trash`, `reduceCost: 1`, exact trait matching, and a conditional hand `Trash` count 2 gated by `ifThisEffectDigivolved`. No module defect was established.

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| Public start-main trigger and legal trash source | Positive tests enter the production turn with `advance.runTurn(0)` and evolve EX11-048 + EX11-005 into EX11-049 from trash | `trigger: "StartOfYourMainPhase"`, `from: ["trash"]`, self target |
| Reduced paid cost and standard draw | Q5791 positive asserts stack identity, final memory `-3` from starting `10` (cost 3 after reduction plus normal turn transition), and the drawn card is then available to the trash count | `reduceCost: 1`, `payCost: true`; ordered `Digivolve` then `Trash` actions |
| Draw-before-trash ordering | Two-card hand leaves one after the bonus draw and trash-2; zero-card hand trashes the bonus draw; one-card hand trashes both the existing card and bonus draw | action order and `ifThisEffectDigivolved` condition |
| Optional refusal | Public turn flow with auto-decline leaves the source in trash, host unchanged, and hand intact | `optional: true`; conditional tail does not execute |
| Exact Dark Dragon/Evil Dragon boundary | BT10-074 Quetzalmon in trash is rejected; a qualifying card in hand is rejected because source is trash-only | exact `match: "trait"`; `from: ["trash"]` |
| Cross-card stack and peer route | A public alternate host stack carrying Yaamon evolves the Evil Dragon peer EX10-053 from trash, preserving the host stack identity and conditional hand cleanup | inherited `StartOfYourMainPhase`, trash source, exact Dark/Evil trait union |
| Controller/timing | Opponent's production main phase does not activate the inherited effect | start-main trigger ownership in production loop |

All fixtures use main-deck Digimon only and contain no Digi-Egg in deck or security.

#### Q&A coverage

- Q5791: covered by the public positive flow; the bonus draw is observed before two hand cards are trashed.
- Q5792: covered by zero-card and one-card hand cases. The one-card case proves the card drawn by evolution is also trashed when possible.

#### Validation

```text
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX11/EX11-005.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
9 passed

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
passed

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-005.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-005.test.ts
passed

pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-005.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX11/EX11-005.test.ts
passed

git diff --check
passed
```

Mutation sensitivity: removing `from: ["trash"]` breaks the hand-source negative; removing `reduceCost: 1` changes the final memory assertion; moving `Trash` before `Digivolve` breaks the Q5791 hand/trash assertions; removing `ifThisEffectDigivolved` causes decline and invalid-trait cases to trash; changing exact trait matching admits BT10-074.

### EX11-006 — Flickmon re-audit

The catalog identifies EX11-006 as the White Digi-Egg Flickmon. Its inherited
text is `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] This Digimon linked with [Maquinamon]
may digivolve into a Digimon card with [Maquinamon] in its text in the hand
with the digivolution cost reduced by 2.`

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX11-006` returned Q5793. The comprehensive
§4-22 rule confirms that “in its text” includes names, effects, inherited text,
rules, evolution requirements, DNA, DigiXros, App Fusion, Link, and Assembly
requirements. The peer EX11-027 tests cover the printed On Play top-three
search, exact Maquinamon category, bottom ordering, and the Tyrannomon/Reptile/
Dinosaur text boundary; this card's focused suite now independently executes
the peer search/link route and consumes the resulting linked Maquinamon.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Printed clause | Behavioural evidence | IR |
|---|---|---|
| `[When Attacking]` | Public attack intents in the positive, no-link, ExMaquinamon, wrong-text, refusal, and once-per-turn tests. | `trigger: "WhenAttacking"` |
| `[Once Per Turn]` | The final test consumes Flickmon once, uses a legal inherited unsuspend peer to attempt a second same-turn attack without a second evolution, then completes an own-to-opponent-to-own public turn loop. The second Turbomon remains in hand after the next-own-turn attack, exposing a same-source re-arm seam. | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| `linked with [Maquinamon]` | Positive linked stack succeeds; no-link and ExMaquinamon exact-name boundary cases leave the hand card untouched. | `selfLinkedMatchesFilter` with exact `nameExact: "Maquinamon"` |
| `may digivolve` | `may decline the linked attack digivolution` uses automatic refusal and asserts host, hand, and memory remain unchanged. | `optional: true` |
| Digivolve into a card with `[Maquinamon]` in its text | Turbomon is accepted through its printed Maquinamon evolution requirement; ForgeBeemon is rejected as wrong text. Q5793's broad text interpretation is used by `match: "text"`. | `into.nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Maquinamon"], match: "text" }]` |
| Hand and cost reduction by 2 | Positive route removes Turbomon from hand, preserves memory because its printed cost 2 is reduced to 0, and asserts the evolved top card plus source stack. | `from: ["hand"]`, `reduceCost: 2`, `payCost: true` |
| Peer search and stack boundary | A public EX11-027 play reveals exact Maquinamon, Maquinamon-text, and nonmatching cards; the exact linked card is placed under the ally while the rest bottoms. | Comparative peer confirms the linked identity consumed by EX11-006. |

The realistic route uses EX11-027 over EX11-006 with an actual linked
Maquinamon, then evolves to Turbomon through the public attack intent. The
focused mixed pool includes exact Maquinamon, ExMaquinamon, a no-link host, a
wrong-text hand card, and a public peer search with exact-name/text/nonmatch
and bottom-order outcomes. The reset probe uses legal main-deck Digimon,
security, and deck cards and no synthetic timing driver. All deck fixtures use
main-deck Digimon; no Digi-Egg appears in a deck or security fixture.

#### Changes

The IR module already encoded the corrected executable gate and remains on the
required exclusive `registerIrCard` path. No card implementation change was
needed. The test file removes the remaining Digi-Egg deck fixture and replaces
the synthetic usage-ledger/fire-timing once-per-turn proof with public attack
and turn-loop intents. EX11-033 is imported only as a legal inherited unsuspend
peer so the same-turn refusal is observable. The focused test imports EX11-027
only to execute its legal public peer search; no peer module is modified.

#### Validation

- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX11-006` — passed; Q5793 recorded.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs rules "X in its text"` — passed; comprehensive §4-22 returned.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX11/EX11-006.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — passed, 8 tests.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — passed.
- Oxlint — passed.
- Oxfmt check — passed after formatting the test file.
- `git diff --check` — passed.

#### Mutation sensitivity and score

Changing the host link filter from exact `nameExact` to substring causes the
ExMaquinamon negative to fail. Removing `match: "text"` or changing the
destination filter causes the ForgeBeemon/wrong-text negative to fail.
Removing `reduceCost: 2` fails the memory assertion, and removing
`frequency: "OncePerTurn"` fails the same-turn refusal assertion.

### EX11-007 — Agumon re-audit

#### Contract

Catalog text: `[Digivolve] [Koromon]: Cost 0`; `[When Moving] [On Play] 1 of your Digimon with [Tyrannomon] in its name or the [Reptile] or [Dinosaur] trait gains Raid and Piercing for the turn`; inherited: `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.` No card-specific KB Q&A is indexed for EX11-007; general rules apply.

The direct module is compiled IR and uses only `registerIrCard("EX11-007", compiled)`. It has full coverage and no residuals.

#### Clause to proof to IR

### EX11-008 — Elizamon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog prints two identical entry effects: `[When Moving] [On Play] 1 of your Digimon with the [Reptile] or [Dragonkin] trait gains ＜Raid＞ and +3000 DP for the turn.` Its inherited clause is `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When your opponent's security stack is removed from, gain 1 memory.` The card is a red level-3 Reptile/LIBERATOR Digimon with a level-2 red evolution cost of 0.

The module is IR-only and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard`. Both entry effects use an exact trait filter, a single target, Raid, and `forTheTurn` +3000 DP. The inherited effect is a `YourTurn` once-per-turn `whenSecurityRemoved` watcher restricted to opponent-owned security. No IR defect was established.

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| On Play target and +3000/Raid | Public play intent selects one of two Reptile peers; selected target moves from 3,000 to 6,000 DP and gains Raid, while the other remains unchanged | `trigger: "OnPlay"`, exact trait filter, `GainKeyword`, `ModifyDP amount: 3000`, `duration: "forTheTurn"` |
| When Moving target and +3000/Raid | Public `moveFromBreeding` intent selects a 3,000 DP Reptile peer and proves the same result | `trigger: "WhenMoving"`, same action pair |
| Public egg/source transition | A legal EX11-008 egg is hatched from `eggDeck` and moved from breeding through the public path before the same ally grant resolves | Digi-Egg source transition into `WhenMoving` |
| Inherited security removal | A real public player attack removes the opponent's top security and the inherited host gains 1 memory | `trigger: "YourTurn"`, inherited `SubTrigger event: "whenSecurityRemoved"`, opponent source filter, `GainMemory 1` |
| Security peer ordering | A public attack removes an opponent security card that plays EX11-057 from Security while the inherited watcher gains memory | public Security play coexists with `whenSecurityRemoved` |
| Exact target and DP boundary | Both entry tests use a 3,000 DP eligible Digimon and assert exactly 6,000 afterward; non-selected peer remains at 1,000 | count 1 + exact trait filter + amount 3000 |
| Once-per-turn/controller boundary | Public opponent-turn removal from the owner's security does not grant memory; the owner-turn public attack and Security peer prove the active source boundary. A same-source repeated attack/reset remains unisolated because the host is suspended after its first attack. | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `YourTurn` |

The tested security fixtures use main-deck Digimon and contain no Digi-Eggs. The breeding fixture hatches legal Digi-Egg BT1-001; EX11-008's public When Moving behavior remains covered by its separate breeding-area fixture. The legal stack uses EX11-009 over EX11-008 for the inherited behavior; the card's ordinary level-2 route is represented by the existing module catalog requirement, while a separate public evolution action was not added.

The card query returns Q5794: Security effects activate before other triggered effects during a security check, and other triggered effects are ordered with the turn player first. ### EX11-012 — Medusamon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog prints Rush and Progress, plus `[When Digivolving] [End of Attack] You may delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with as much or less DP as this Digimon. Then, by returning 1 card from your opponent's trash to the bottom of the deck, they play 1 [Petrification] Token.` The token reminder text supplies its own `[Your Turn] This Digimon can't suspend` and On Deletion security behavior. Medusamon also prints `[On Deletion] Trash your top security card` and `[All Turns] When this Digimon would leave the battle area, by deleting 1 Token, it doesn't leave.`

The module is full IR and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard`. It correctly keeps token reminder behavior out of Medusamon, models both When Digivolving and End of Attack, uses opponent-trash → deck-bottom as a paid return cost, places the token as an opponent Digimon, and uses an `isSelfRef` leave-play replacement with token deletion cost. No card IR defect was established.

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| Rush / Progress | Play-on-turn test asserts both keywords and immediately attacks by public intent | Static keyword actions |
| When Digivolving delete + token | Public evolution deletes a target within source DP, returns opponent trash to deck bottom, and creates opponent-controlled Petrification Token | `WhenDigivolving`, relative DP delete, `PlayToken placedAs: opponentDigimon`, deckBottom return cost |
| DP boundary / no legal target | Large-DP opponent survives while token tail continues | `dp: {op: lte, relativeToSource: true}` |
| End of Attack | Public attack to player resolves the same delete/return/token sequence | `EndOfAttack` duplicate action sequence |
| Q5801 play lock | Crimson Blaze play lock still permits this effect to play the token | `PlayToken` is effect-controlled and independent of ordinary play restriction |
| Q6045 immediate removal | Reactor interaction removes Medusamon during the first process; the token tail still resolves | sequential effect continuation after source leaves |
| Leave replacement | Effect deletion, rule deletion, return-to-hand, and opponent token payment are covered; available token is deleted and Medusamon remains | `AllTurns` `Replacement event: wouldLeavePlay`, delete-own-token cost, prevent leave |
| Q6514 repeated rule checks | Two tokens are consumed across repeated 0-DP checks and the next deletion proceeds after tokens are exhausted | replacement watcher can react again after each new rule check |

All deck and security fixtures use main-deck Digimon; no Digi-Egg is used in deck or security. Public play, evolution, attack, and security-related paths are exercised. The explicit deletion/return-to-hand replacement tests use the named testkit leave/delete seam because no ordinary public card action in this fixture creates each required leave event; they are retained as observable engine-seam tests rather than timing claims.

- Q5800: positive evolution test asserts the token is controlled by the opponent seat while activated by the Medusamon controller.
- Q5801: Crimson Blaze test proves the effect-created token still plays under the play lock.

- Q6514: repeated 0-DP rule-check test covers reactivation after a token is deleted.

### EX11-016 — PolarBearmon re-audit

The catalog identifies EX11-016 as the Blue/Yellow level 5 PolarBearmon with
the Ice-Snow and LIBERATOR traits. Its alternate route is level 4 with
Ice-Snow for cost 3. Printed behavior is Iceclad; On Play/When Digivolving,
trash any 2 opposing digivolution cards, then optionally place one of the
opponent's Digimon with no digivolution cards as top or bottom security; and
the inherited Your Turn condition grants the Ice-Snow host Piercing and
Security Attack +1 while the opponent has no Digimon with digivolution cards.

The card query returned Q5802 and Q6046. Q5802 is proven by the battle test
where deleting the opponent's final stacked Digimon makes Piercing available
at the same timing and the attack performs the expected checks. Q6046 is proven
by the empty-opponent board in the inherited-grant test. The rules query
returned comprehensive §16-35 for Iceclad.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Printed clause | Behavioural evidence | IR |
|---|---|---|
| Ice-Snow level-4 alternate route, cost 3 | Public `digivolve` from EX11-015 asserts top-card transition and memory 3 -> 0. Normal Blue/Yellow level-4 routes cost 4; red BT1-015 is rejected. | Alternate requirement `{level: 4, traits: ["Ice-Snow"], cost: 3}`. |
| Iceclad | A real PolarBearmon stack with two sources defeats a higher-DP opponent with fewer sources, proving source-count comparison. | Static `IceClad` keyword. |
| Trash any 2 opposing digivolution cards | Two opposing stacks are emptied across the stacks and exactly two cards enter trash; refusal test proves this mandatory step still resolves. | `TrashDigivolution`, amount 2, `scope: "acrossDigimon"`, opponent Digimon with sources. |
| Optional security placement | Public play and public digivolution cover top placement; bottom placement is selected and order asserted; new refusal test keeps the source-free Digimon in battle and security unchanged. | Optional `SecurityManipulation.addTopOrBottom`, opponent source filter `digivolutionCards: "none"`, face-down. |
| Inherited Piercing and Security Attack +1 | Own Ice-Snow host gains both when opponent has no stacked Digimon, including empty opponent; non-Ice-Snow host, stacked opponent, and opponent turn withhold both. | Two inherited `YourTurn` auras, exact Ice-Snow self filter and opponent `opponentHasNone` source condition. |
| Q5802 transition timing | Battle deletes the last source-bearing opponent and then asserts Piercing is active and security is checked. | Continuous condition re-evaluates after stack deletion. |

The fixtures use legal main-deck Digimon in deck/security and realistic
evolution stacks. No injected timing calls are used. The positive and refusal
paths assert exact permanent IDs, source stack lengths, security order/count,
memory, and settled attack state.

#### Changes

The module was already faithful and retains exclusive `registerIrCard`; no IR
change was needed. The test file gained a public refusal case for the optional
security placement after the mandatory two-card trash, proving the exact
boundary between mandatory and optional processing. Existing tests cover both
On Play and When Digivolving paths, top/bottom placement, Iceclad, the
alternate route, ordinary peers, and Q5802/Q6046.

### EX11-018 — Ryugumon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog defines blue level-6 Aquatic Ryugumon with Evade and Decode (level 5 or lower with Aqua/Sea Animal in any trait). Its entry clause is `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] By placing 1 Digimon card with [Aqua] or [Sea Animal] in any of its traits from your hand as this Digimon's bottom digivolution card, 1 of your Digimon unsuspends.` Its all-turns clause is once per turn: when effects add cards to this Digimon's stack, return one opponent Digimon with as many or fewer digivolution cards as this Digimon to the bottom deck.

The module is full IR and exclusively uses `registerIrCard`. It uses `traitContains` for Aqua/Sea Animal, places the paid card at the bottom of the self stack, shares the printed once-per-turn use key across On Play/When Digivolving/When Attacking, scopes Decode to the self stack and level <=5, and compares opponent stack counts against the source.

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| Aquatic paid placement and selected unsuspend | Public `playCard` of Ryugumon places an Aquatic card at the bottom and unsuspends the preferred ally; the observable source stack, hand, and opponent deck endpoints are asserted | shared `Unsuspend` + `place` cost, `traitContains`, bottom/self host |
| Optional refusal and invalid peer | Decline test preserves hand/stack; non-Aqua/Sea Animal hand card does nothing | optional abortable cost and exact traitContains boundary |
| Three printed timings / shared OPT | Mechanism test exercises On Play, When Digivolving, and When Attacking and proves one shared stack placement/use. The added public turn-loop probe plays Ryugumon, returns to the next own turn, and attacks with distinct legal BT10-023 and BT1-033 costs; the second cost leaves hand, is placed under the source, and unsuspends the source. | three triggers, `OncePerTurn`, `sharedUseKey` |
| Add-source watcher | Public testkit placement adds sources and returns only an opponent with <= source count; second use is refused; additions under a neighbor are ignored | `onAddDigivolutionCards`, `digivolutionCardsCompareToSource: lte`, self source filter |
| Decode scope and level/battle boundaries | Own level-5 Aqua-containing source decodes; level-6 and battle deletion do not; neighbor source cannot be decoded | self `hostFilter`, `from: digivolutionCards`, `leaveCause: otherThanBattle` |
| Evade | Q6515/Q6516 tests accept Evade, suspend the source, prevent first deletion, then allow Decode/next rule deletion | Static Evade plus production evade response |
| Aquatic metadata and ordinary evolution | Catalog test asserts Aquatic; final test proves blue level-5 cost 3 and rejects off-color level 5 | catalog requirement and public digivolve intent |

Fixtures use main-deck Digimon for incidental cards and no Digi-Egg in hand, deck, or security. Source-stack cards are deliberately stack materials. The primary On Play proof now uses public play; the remaining timing-specific and leave/add-source cases retain named production testkit drivers because no minimal public fixture creates those events without changing shared infrastructure. They assert observable zones and decisions rather than synthetic timing success.

- Q6515: Decode, Evade, prevention, and repeated rule deletion are tested.
- Q6516: Evade-first then Decode is tested in the same deletion window.
- Q5809–Q5815, Q5822–Q5824: these KB-index entries belong to adjacent EX11 cards and are not attributed to EX11-018.

hand without paying; and inherited Opponent's Turn, once per turn, when an
opponent Digimon attacks, optionally delete one of your other Digimon to end
that attack.

### EX11-023 — Kaguyamon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog defines yellow/purple level-6 Puppet Kaguyamon with normal level-5 yellow or purple evolution cost 4 and alternate level-5 Puppet cost 3. Printed keywords are Alliance and Scapegoat. Its `[When Digivolving] [End of Opponent's Turn] [Once Per Turn]` effect deletes one opponent Digimon with the lowest level. Its `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` effect, when another Digimon is deleted, may play one level-4-or-lower Puppet from trash without paying its cost.

The module is full IR and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard`. It shares the once-per-turn key between When Digivolving and End of Opponent's Turn, uses lowest-level targeting, excludes self from the deletion watcher, and gates recursive play to level <=4 Puppet cards from trash. No IR defect was established.

| Clause | Behavioral proof | IR mapping |
|---|---|---|
| Keywords | Alliance prompt test observes and resolves an ally selection; Scapegoat test deletes another Digimon and preserves Kaguyamon | Static Alliance/Scapegoat keywords |
| Puppet alternate / ordinary evolution | Public evolution covers normal yellow/purple cost 4, Puppet alternate cost 3, and rejects an invalid level-3 peer | evolution requirements and catalog normal costs |
| Lowest-level deletion | Public digivolution deletes EX11-019 while retaining higher-level EX11-021 | `superlative: lowestLevel`, opponent Digimon |
| End-of-opponent-turn timing / shared OPT | The end-of-opponent-turn boundary now runs through the production `runOneTurn` / `endPhase` loop for both opponent and own-turn cases; the shared-use test retains explicit timing coverage for same-turn suppression | `EndOfOpponentsTurn`, `OncePerTurn`, shared key |
| Recursive Puppet play | Other-Digimon deletion plays level-4 Puppet from trash; decline, level/trait misses, self-deletion, and once-per-turn refusal are covered | `onDeletionOf`, `excludeSelf`, trash source, level <=4, exact Puppet trait |

Fixtures contain no Digi-Egg in deck or security. The focused evolution scenarios use legal Puppet and yellow/purple peers. End-of-turn evidence uses the production turn loop; the shared-use test retains a named timing driver because the current loop stops in `End` and exposes no public transition into the next `Draw` phase. The public digivolution, attack/Alliance, and Scapegoat paths provide behavioral evidence.

The applicable general mechanisms are covered by public lowest-level deletion, recursive deletion trigger, Alliance, and Scapegoat tests. Same-turn and next-turn reset of every once-per-turn branch remains a named gap where the minimal public fixture lacks a natural repeated timing window.

### EX11-026 — Pteromon audit

#### Contract and IR

The catalog defines green level-3 Pteromon with a green level-2 evolution cost of 0. Its entry text is `[When Moving] [On Play] You may suspend 1 Digimon. If this effect suspended your Digimon, 1 of your Digimon with [Avian] or [Bird] in any of its traits or the [Vortex Warriors] trait gets +3000 DP until your opponent's turn ends.` The inherited text is `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon wins a battle, gain 1 memory.`

The module is full IR and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard`. Both entry timings allow any-player suspension, then gate the +3000 bonus on the effect having suspended the controller's own Digimon. #### Follow-up seam investigation

The original case had two fixture problems. It omitted the public `chooseOption` decision, so
EX11-034's When Attacking effect stopped at its legal top/bottom security choice. It also used a
low-cost target, so EX11-034's own 8-cost DeleteBudget trashed the target before battle and never
published `whenDeletesInBattle`. The corrected fixture enables `autoChooseOption` and attacks a
12-cost EX11-034 target with a 13,000-DP EX11-034/EX11-033 host; the target therefore survives the
printed deletion effect, loses the real battle, and enters trash. The inherited watcher then
unsuspends the host.

The production trace confirms the public payload and identity path: `resolveDigimonBattle` emits
`subjectPermanentId`/`attackerPermanentId` for the surviving host, `buildSubTriggerSourceContext`
rebinds the inherited EX11-033 source instance to `sourcePermanentId` on that host, and
`armedSubTriggers` matches the existing `whenDeletesInBattle` self gate. No engine correction is
indicated; changing subscription matching would be an unnecessary card-specific workaround.

Q5850 is separate from this battle continuation. It concerns rule-check ordering when a linked
Maquinamon play leaves its host at 0 DP before the play's On Play effect can restore it. The
existing cross-card probe uses direct timing injection and therefore is useful diagnostic evidence
only; it is not public behavioral credit for EX11-033. The rule-check result remains separately
documented, with no new synthetic probe added here.

Q5850's public production proof and a real opponent-turn-end restriction expiry remain harness
seams. Existing synthetic `whenLinked`/`whenDeletesInBattle` calls were removed from behavioral
credit.

Its All Turns Once Per Turn watcher is caused by any of the controller's Digimon suspending and may play one green Digimon with 3000 DP or less and Avian or Bird in any trait from hand without cost. The play ceiling gains 2000 for each suspended Digimon.

The module uses only `registerIrCard`. The local correction adds the missing normal green level-5 evolution requirement and changes the Avian/Bird target to `traitContains`, so traits such as Bird Dragon and Giant Bird satisfy the printed partial-trait wording. The unsuspend/suspend targets and suspension scaling remain cross-player (`controllerDefault: "any"`), while the trigger source remains the controller's own Digimon.

|---|---|---|

| Q5857 and optional sequence | Public evolution test selects the opponent's suspended Digimon for unsuspend, then the owner's host for suspend; final suspension states are asserted | optional Unsuspend followed by optional Suspend, both `controllerDefault: "any"` |
| All Turns trigger and hand play | Public suspension of an own Digimon plays EX11-026 from hand and leaves the nonmatching red/non-trait cards in hand | `SubTrigger(event: "whenSuspended")` and `PlayWithoutCost` |
| Partial traits and green/DP boundary | EX11-026's Bird Dragon proves substring trait matching; structural target asserts green and base 3000 DP ceiling | `traitContains`, `colors: ["Green"]`, and `dp <= 3000` |
| Suspended-Digimon scaling | A legal public play isolates the live 4000-DP Giant Bird boundary: with an opponent's Digimon already suspended and the own trigger then suspended, BT16-008 is played under the raised 7000-DP ceiling | `dpCeilingModifier.raiseCeiling`, 2000 per suspended Digimon, controller any |
| Once-per-turn reset | A real production turn loop suspends two different own Digimon across consecutive own turns and plays one Avian on each cycle | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

The peer check uses EX11-026's matching and public suspension patterns, and the evolution case uses a real level-5 green stack. The mechanism regression covers dynamic DP ceiling materialization before optional loose-card preflight while retaining live-board counting. No Digi-Egg is placed in a deck or security zone, no numeric security shorthand is used, and no injected timing call is used for the new evolution or trigger tests. The existing direct `advance.verb.suspend` calls are public testkit intents.

Pyramidimon is a level 6 Black Mineral/LIBERATOR Digimon. Its catalog clauses are:

- `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` when an effect trashes any of this Digimon's digivolution cards, optionally place 3 `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` cards from trash as this Digimon's bottom digivolution cards.

| When Attacking paid deletion | Public attack on the fully assembled stack resolves the same deletion and recovery path. | `WhenAttacking` carries the same `sharedUseKey` and `frequency: OncePerTurn`. |
| Exact 3-card cost and mixed stacks | The two-stack public case pays two cards from Pyramidimon and one from an ally; the two-card negative case refuses the effect without trashing or deleting. | Cost count is 3, `zone: "digivolutionCards"`, `from: ["digivolutionCards"]`, and Mineral/Rock trait matching. |
| Highest-play-cost boundary | Mixed opponent Digimon targets prove the higher-cost permanent is deleted while the lower-cost one survives. | Target uses `superlative: "highestPlayCost"`, `kind: ["Digimon", "Tamer"]`. |
| All Turns recovery | Public attacks and evolution cause the effect-driven stack trash, then the watcher returns all 3 Mineral cards from trash to the source's bottom stack. | `AllTurns` subtrigger `whenDigivolutionTrashed`, self/by-effect source filter, optional `PlaceUnder` from trash count 3 at `position: "bottom"`. |

The evolution path uses BT10-064 Gogmamon, an exact Rock level-5 peer, and asserts that the pre-existing base remains beneath the three recovered Mineral cards. No Digi-Egg is used in deck or security. No unrelated Mineral/Rock filter or synthetic timing was introduced.

### EX11-045 — Metatromon audit

#### Contract and implementation

The catalog defines black level-6 Metatromon, play cost 12, 12000 DP, Machine/LIBERATOR, and the alternate Assembly -5 recipe requiring five black Digimon cards with [Maquinamon] in their text. It also has an alternate level-5 [Maquinamon] in text evolution for cost 3 and Blocker. Its On Play, When Digivolving, and When Attacking once-per-turn effects de-digivolve one opposing Digimon by 2, then restrict one opposing Digimon or Tamer from digivolving until that opponent's turn ends. At the end of the owner's turn, once per turn, another Digimon may free-digivolve into a green Maquinamon-text card from hand. The inherited All Turns once-per-turn effect deletes the opposing Digimon with the lowest play cost when an effect adds cards to this Digimon's digivolution cards.

The module is exclusive `registerIrCard`, full coverage, and retains the compiled `onAddDigivolutionCards` SubTrigger for the inherited effect. It encodes exact Maquinamon-in-text matching, the five-card black Assembly recipe, Blocker metadata, the three shared once-per-turn effect windows, green hand evolution, and lowest-play-cost deletion.

| Blocker | Live field keyword observation confirms printed Blocker | Static keyword node |
| De-Digivolve/restriction | Public play and digivolution flows reduce a three-card opponent stack by 2 and expose the digivolve restriction; a public attack flow reaches the shared effect resolution, while its target board is consumed before a stable restriction observation | On Play/When Digivolving/When Attacking actions |
| End-of-turn evolution | Public turn loop evolves another Digimon from EX11-027 into green Maquinamon-text EX11-029 from hand at end of turn; the gauge changes perspective from 3 to -3 at turn handoff and emits no digivolution-payment event | EndOfYourTurn free Digivolve |
| Inherited deletion | Public BT19-024 On Play placement adds BT19-021 beneath the host carrying EX11-045; opposing lowest-cost BT1-009 is deleted while BT1-010 remains | AllTurns inherited `onAddDigivolutionCards` SubTrigger |

Q5893 is reconciled by the `texts: ["Maquinamon"]` matcher used by the evolution and Assembly recipe; this includes names, traits, effects, inherited text, requirements, and related printed text. Q5894 is exercised through the public BT19-024 placement producer and remains distinct from a Link event. The end-of-turn public proof verifies free evolution by asserting that no `memoryChanged` event with reason `digivolve` occurs; the apparent sign change is the normal gauge-perspective handoff. The On Play and When Digivolving routes retain and assert the public digivolve restriction. The When Attacking route proves the public timing reaches EX11-045 resolution.

Fixtures use ordinary main-deck cards only. No Digi-Egg appears in security or deck, no numeric security shorthand is used, and the test contains no injected timing APIs, `it.fails`, or skipped cases.

All Turns grants Scapegoat to all own Dark Dragon/Evil Dragon Digimon while the controller has four or fewer cards in hand. ### EX11-051 — — Necromon

Necromon is a level 6 Purple Ghost/LIBERATOR Digimon. Its catalog clauses are:

- `＜Piercing＞` and `＜Execute＞`;
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [On Deletion]` delete one opponent's lowest-level Digimon, then optionally play a level 4 or lower `[Ghost]` from trash for free;
- a second `[On Deletion]` effect may free-digivolve one of your `[Ghost]` Digimon into a `[Ghost]` card from hand.

Q5904 asks about the order of the two simultaneous On Deletion effects. Q5905 covers a pending On Deletion effect leaving trash through a DemiMeramon interaction.

#### Clause → public test → IR

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Piercing and Execute | Structural catalog/compiled assertions verify both keywords. | Separate static keyword entries for `Piercing` and `Execute`. |
| On Play lowest-level deletion | Publicly plays Necromon from hand with level 3 and level 4 opposing Digimon; the level 3 target is deleted and the level 4 remains. | `OnPlay` begins with opponent Digimon filter and `superlative: "lowestLevel"`, count 1. |
| On Play Ghost recovery | The same public play selects BT20-063 Ghost from trash and places it in the battle area without cost. | Optional `PlayWithoutCost`, from trash, Ghost trait, level `lte 4`. |
| Lowest-level tie boundary | Public play with two tied level 3 opponents deletes exactly one and leaves the higher level Digimon plus the other tied target. | Target count remains 1 with lowest-level superlative. |
| Trait and level boundary | Public play with level 5 Ghost, level 3 non-Ghost, and level 4 Ghost plays only the level 4 Ghost; the other cards remain in trash. | Exact Ghost trait filter and level comparison `lte 4`. |
| Optional recovery refusal | Public play with `autoDeclineOptional` still deletes the lowest-level opponent while the Ghost remains in trash. | `optional: true` on the recovery action after deletion. |
| When Digivolving | Existing public test coverage was replaced by a legal public play path for the shared deletion/recovery clause; the compiled trigger is structurally checked for the same action sequence. | `WhenDigivolving` mirrors the On Play Delete and optional Ghost play actions. |
| On Deletion effects | A public opponent attack deletes suspended Necromon. Its first deletion effect removes the opponent's surviving attacker and plays a Ghost from trash; its second effect free-digivolves another Ghost into BT11-078 Soulmon from hand. | Two `OnDeletion` entries: Delete + trash Ghost play, then optional free `Digivolve` from hand into a Ghost host. |

#### Peer and stack evidence

All behavioral fixtures use legal main-deck Digimon. The positive On Deletion flow uses BT20-063 Ghost as the host, BT4-080 Ghost in trash, and BT11-078 Soulmon in hand, proving both Ghost trait routes across trash and hand. The public attack uses a high-DP BT1-080 attacker against a suspended Necromon, so deletion is resolved through battle rather than an injected delete verb. No Digi-Egg is used in deck or security.

The engine exposes the two card-source trigger keys; the two Necromon IR entries are grouped under one source key, so no unsupported synthetic sub-order is claimed. Q5905 is proven by returning Necromon from trash before its pending effects activate; the opponent attacker remains and no Necromon deletion effect resolves.

The catalog defines a blue/yellow cost-4 LIBERATOR Tamer. At the start of the owner's main phase, if the opponent has a Digimon, it gains 1 memory. On Play, for each own Ice-Snow Digimon, it trashes any one digivolution card from an opposing Digimon. All Turns, when an effect trashes digivolution cards from an opposing Digimon, by suspending this Tamer it gains 1 memory. Its Security effect plays this card without paying the cost.

The module is exclusive `registerIrCard`, full coverage, and encodes opponent-presence memory, pooled Ice-Snow scaling, cross-Digimon trashing, the paid suspension watcher, and Security play.

#### Evidence

| Clause | Proof | IR |
|---|---|---|
| Catalog and IR | Structural test checks metadata, full coverage, On Play pooled scaling, and the All Turns paid watcher | compiled effect tree |
| On Play trash scaling | Public play with both Ice-Snow trait variants trashes one source from each opposing stack; the one-Ice-Snow boundary leaves one source and the near-match leaves its source intact | TrashDigivolution amount 1, acrossDigimon scaling |
| No Ice-Snow boundary | Public play with no Ice-Snow Digimon leaves the opposing stack unchanged | scaling resolves zero |
| All Turns watcher and exact source boundary | A public inherited EX9-015 effect trashes an opponent's digivolution card; Suzune suspends and gains 1 memory through the real `whenDigivolutionTrashed` event. A public decline leaves the stack change intact but refuses the suspend and memory payment. | AllTurns `whenDigivolutionTrashed` SubTrigger |
| Start Main | A production turn loop reaches the owner's main phase with an opposing Digimon present and gains 1 memory | `StartOfYourMainPhase` |
| Security | A production turn loop attacks a player's security and publicly plays Suzune from the checked security card without paying its cost | `Security` `PlayWithoutCost` |

There are no card-specific Q&A entries for EX11-057 in the local KB; general rules apply. Fixtures use legal ordinary main-deck cards only; Digi-Egg cards, illegal security/deck fixtures, numeric security shorthand, injected timing APIs, `it.fails`, and skipped tests are absent.

### EX11-058 — — Yao Qinglan

Yao Qinglan is a blue LIBERATOR Tamer. Its catalog clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` by placing one level 5 or lower card with `[Aqua]` or `[Sea Animal]` in any trait from hand as the bottom digivolution card of one matching Digimon, gain 1 memory;
- `[All Turns]` when one of your Digimon is played or digivolves and has `[Aqua]` or `[Sea Animal]` in any trait, by suspending this Tamer, draw 1; if played by `<Decode>`, one opposing Digimon cannot suspend until its turn ends;
- `[Security]` play this card without paying its cost.

Q5911 defines the Decode provenance condition. Q5912 distinguishes a Decode play trigger from a later digivolution trigger.

#### Clause → public test → IR

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Start-main placement and memory | Public turn-loop tests place a legal level 5-or-lower Aqua/Aquatic card under a matching Digimon and assert memory +1 and bottom stack position. | `StartOfYourMainPhase` optional place cost from hand, level `lte 5`, matching trait, bottom position, then `GainMemory(1)`. |
| Trait substring semantics | Public Aquatic placement succeeds; Sea Beast placement remains in hand and memory unchanged. | Uses `traitContains` for Aqua and Sea Animal, with explicit OR union. |
| Level boundary | Public level 6 Aquatic payment remains in hand, does not alter the host stack, and gains no memory. | Placement cost requires `levelComparison: lte 5`. |
| Played watcher | Publicly plays Aquatic and Aqua/Sea Animal Digimon from hand. Owen suspends and draws after the matching play; the Sea Beast play does not trigger. | `whenPlayed` watcher has exact union, suspend self cost, optional Draw, and Decode-only Restrict. |
| Digivolved watcher | Structural IR assertion confirms a separate `whenOneOfYoursDigivolves` watcher with Draw only; its public behavior is covered by the same watcher contract and no Decode restriction is attached. | Digivolve watcher carries the suspend-cost Draw and no Restrict action. |
| Suspend refusal / Q5911 boundary | Public `autoDeclineOptional` play leaves Yao unsuspended and draws nothing. A normal public play proves the Decode restriction does not fire without Decode provenance. | Draw is optional with `abortOnDecline`; Restrict condition is `triggerPlayedByDecode`. |
| Public Decode provenance / Q5911-Q5912 | A public EX11-050 effect deletes EX11-018 and its Decode replacement publicly plays the stacked BT14-008; the test asserts the replacement card is present in the battle area while the EX11-018 host remains. The decoded card reaches play with `playedByDecode: true`; the combined entry window drains Yao's nested `whenPlayed` watcher, suspending Yao and restricting the opponent. The later public digivolution case correctly draws without restriction. | Decode replacement uses `playedByDecode: true`; Yao gates Restrict on `triggerPlayedByDecode`. |
| Security play | Public opponent security attack checks Yao from security and places it in the battle area without payment. | Security effect is self `PlayWithoutCost`, marked `isSecurity`. |

#### Peer and boundary evidence

The placement tests use BT14-008/BT2-024 matching Aqua-family Digimon and legal main-deck payment cards. The watcher tests use matching Aquatic cards and a nonmatching Sea Beast peer. Level and trait boundaries are asserted with legal main-deck cards. No Digi-Egg is placed in deck or security.

#### Q&A coverage

Q5911 is covered by the public non-Decode play refusal and by the public Decode producer route, which now resolves the provenance-gated restriction. Q5912 is covered by public normal play and later digivolution: the digivolution draws but does not apply the Decode restriction. The producer is EX11-018's public Decode replacement, reached through EX11-050's public On Play deletion.

### EX11-061 — — Mirai Kinosaki

Mirai Kinosaki is a yellow/purple LIBERATOR Tamer with play cost 4. Its catalog clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` if the opponent has a Digimon, gain 1 memory;
- `[Your Turn]` when one of your Digimon digivolves into a `[Puppet]` trait Digimon, by suspending this Tamer, you may play one level 3 `[Puppet]` trait Digimon from hand without paying its cost; at turn end, delete the Digimon this effect played;
- `[Security]` play this card without paying its cost.

Q5915 confirms that Mirai deletes the Digimon played by her effect at turn end. Q5916 identifies the turn-end processing-order question; this audit proves the real turn-end deletion but does not claim a complete alternate-order matrix.

#### Clause → public test → IR

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Start-main condition and memory | A public turn loop with an opposing Digimon reaches seat 0's main phase and asserts memory +1. | `StartOfYourMainPhase` uses an opponent-controlled `Digimon` filter and `GainMemory(1)`. |
| Puppet digivolution watcher | A public digivolution into EX11-021 fires the watcher, suspends Mirai, and plays EX11-020 from hand without cost. | `YourTurn` contains `whenOneOfYoursDigivolves` with an own Digimon source filter requiring the `Puppet` trait. |
| Exact level/trait hand target | A mixed hand puts level 4 Puppet EX11-021 and unrelated BT1-090 before legal level 3 Puppet EX11-020; only EX11-020 is played. | `PlayWithoutCost` filters hand Digimon to `levels: [3]` and `Puppet` trait, with count 1. |
| Optional suspend cost | A public `autoDeclineOptional` digivolution leaves Mirai unsuspended, leaves both hand cards in hand, and leaves the existing Puppet alive. | Play action is optional, has self suspend cost, and `abortOnDecline`. |
| Turn-end deletion / Q5915 | The played EX11-020 is deleted only after a real main-phase transition into the opponent's turn; the evolved EX11-021 remains. | `DelayedDelete` is inside the digivolution watcher after `PlayWithoutCost`, targeting the permanent played by this effect. |
| Security play | A legal public attack checks EX11-061 from security and places Mirai in the battle area without payment. | Security effect is self `PlayWithoutCost` and marked `isSecurity`. |

#### Peer and boundary evidence

The digivolution tests use a real EX11-020 Puppet host and EX11-021 Puppet evolution, with a level-4 Puppet and a non-Puppet peer in hand. The decline case proves no suspend, no play, and no delayed deletion when the optional cost is refused. Main-deck cards are used for all fixtures; no Digi-Egg is placed in a deck or security stack.

#### Q&A coverage

Q5915 is covered by the public turn transition and exact-card trash assertion. Q5916 is covered by two public turn-end runs with BT13-099's independent End of Your Turn processing ordered before and after Mirai's delayed delete. Both orders delete only the EX11-020 Puppet played by Mirai; the evolved EX11-021 host and BT13-099 remain. No synthetic timing API, injected trigger, `it.fails`, or skip is used.

### EX11-065 — — Close

Close is a black LIBERATOR Tamer with play cost 3. Its catalog clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` by trashing one `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` trait card from hand or one of your Digimon's digivolution cards, gain 1 memory;
- `[All Turns]` when your Digimon are played or digivolve, if any of them have the `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` trait, by suspending this Tamer, you may place one `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` trait card from hand or trash as the bottom digivolution card of any of those Digimon;
- `[Security]` play this card without paying its cost.

No EX11-065-specific local KB Q&A were found. The implementation remains exclusively registered through `registerIrCard`.

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Start-main trash and memory | A real turn loop reaches seat 0's main phase with EX8-051 in a host stack; Close trashes it and gains 1 memory. | `StartOfYourMainPhase` has a Mineral/Rock trait cost from hand or `digivolutionCards`, followed by `GainMemory(1)`. |
| Played Mineral/Rock watcher | Public `playCard` flows cover Rock BT13-061 and Mineral BT10-062 sources, with hand cards placed under the newly played source. A second eligible source proves the placement binds to the trigger source. | `AllTurns` has `whenPlayed` with an own Digimon Mineral/Rock source filter and bottom `PlaceUnder` from hand/trash. |
| Digivolution watcher and bottom placement | A public BT13-061 → BT10-062 digivolution triggers placement from trash; the material is asserted at stack index 0 beneath the existing card. | Separate `whenOneOfYoursDigivolves` watcher uses the same trait filter and `position: "bottom"`. |
| Exact card pools and trait boundaries | Public flows cover hand and trash pools, a nonmatching Option remains in hand, and the start-main cost uses a real evolution-stack Mineral. Declining the suspend cost leaves Close unsuspended; a second same-turn trigger cannot pay while it remains suspended. | Both costs use exact Mineral/Rock trait matching, `from: ["hand", "trash"]`, optional suspend cost, and `abortOnDecline`. |
| Security play | A legal public attack checks EX11-065 from security and places it in the battle area without payment. | Security effect is self `PlayWithoutCost` with `isSecurity: true`. |

Tests use legal main-deck Digimon and trait cards for public play and evolution, with a mixed non-trait Option boundary. Rock and Mineral positives cover hand, trash, and evolution-stack pools; a second eligible source proves trigger-source binding, and a second same-turn event proves the suspend cost is required again. No Digi-Egg is inserted directly into a battle stack, deck, or security; although Rock Digi-Eggs exist in the catalog, no legal public hatch/move sequence exposes this Tamer's play/digivolve watcher without adding an unrelated timing seam. The structural filter assertion preserves the card-text requirement that target cards are cards rather than Digimon-only targets.

### EX11-066 — Xeno audit

#### Contract and implementation

The catalog defines Xeno as a black, cost-4 LIBERATOR Tamer. Its Rule also treats its name as [Zenith]. At the start of its owner's main phase and on play, the controller may trash one card with [Vemmon] in its text from hand to draw 1 and gain 1 memory. During all turns, when one of the controller's Vemmon Digimon is played or digivolves, the controller may suspend Xeno to reveal the top two cards of the deck, place every revealed card with [Vemmon] in its name under that triggering Digimon, and trash the rest. Security plays Xeno without paying its cost.

The module uses exclusive `registerIrCard`, has full coverage, and encodes the Rule alias, both draw/memory triggers, exact Vemmon-in-text matching, both public watcher events, optional self-suspension, reveal/add/rest dispositions, and Security play.

### EX11-068 — — Violet Inboots

Violet Inboots is a purple LIBERATOR Tamer with play cost 4. Its catalog clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Turn]` if memory is 2 or less, set it to 3;
- `[Your Turn]` when one of your `[Ghost]` trait Digimon attacks, by suspending this Tamer, draw 1 and trash 1 card from hand; if attacking by `<Execute>`, that Digimon may digivolve into a `[Ghost]` trait Digimon card in hand with its digivolution cost reduced by 2;
- `[Security]` play this card without paying its cost.

Q5938 defines “if attacking by `<Execute>`” as an attack triggered by the `<Execute>` mechanic. Q6517 concerns a different EX11-062 suspension follow-up and is not applicable to this card.

#### Clause → public test → IR

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Start-of-turn memory floor | A real turn loop begins at memory 2 and asserts memory becomes 3, then closes the phase and surrenders the next seat. | `StartOfYourTurn` uses `memoryAtMost: 2` and `SetMemory(3)`. |
| Ghost attack watcher and suspend cost | A public `<Execute>` end-of-turn attack by BT20-072 triggers Violet; she suspends, draws, and trashes a hand card. | `YourTurn` contains `whenAttacking` with own Digimon and exact `Ghost` trait filter, self suspend cost, Draw 1, and hand Trash. |
| Non-Execute and non-Ghost boundaries | A public attack by Ghost BT20-063 receives draw/trash but remains unevolved because it lacks Execute. A public non-Ghost attack leaves Violet and hand unchanged. | Digivolution is separately gated by `triggerAttackBy: Execute`; the watcher source requires `Ghost`. |
| Execute digivolution clause | The public Execute attack proves Violet's suspend/draw/trash response and the reduced-cost digivolution: BT20-072 evolves into EX11-051, which is then observed in trash after Execute's printed self-deletion. A Ghost BT20-063 attack remains unevolved. | `Digivolve.target.sourceRef` is `triggerSubject`, target card requires Ghost, `reduceCost: 2`, and condition is Q5938's Execute trigger. |
| Security play | A legal public security attack checks EX11-068 and places it in the battle area without payment. | Security effect is self `PlayWithoutCost` with `isSecurity: true`. |

#### Peer and stack evidence

The public tests use BT20-072 (Ghost + Execute), BT20-063 (Ghost without Execute), and EX11-049 (non-Ghost) as meaningful attacker boundaries. The positive Execute flow proves the real end-of-turn Execute attack and Violet's draw/trash response, while observing the evolved EX11-051 in trash after the legal Execute self-deletion. The non-Execute Ghost boundary remains negative.

All fixtures use legal main-deck cards. No Digi-Egg is placed in a deck, security, or battle stack.

#### Q&A coverage

Q5938 is covered publicly for the Execute attack's Violet response and positively by the reduced-cost Ghost digivolution. The real BT20-063 non-Execute Ghost attack remains negative. The traced shared seam was `CombatController.resolveAttack`: it already constructed `attackTrigger.attackMechanic = "Execute"`, but omitted that field from `attackSubTriggerPayload`, so `triggerAttackBy` could not see it in prepared or nested `whenAttacking` contexts. The fix copies the field into the shared payload. A focused controller regression asserts the payload preserves `Execute`, while EX11-068 proves the public card outcome. Q6517 is unrelated to EX11-068.

Security plays Yuuki without paying its cost.

### EX11-073 — ExMaquinamon audit

#### Contract and implementation

The catalog defines ExMaquinamon as a green/black level-7 Unique LIBERATOR Digimon with DNA digivolution from green level 6 plus black level 6 for cost 0. It has Security Attack +1, Blocker, and Link +2. When DNA digivolving, it may link up to three exact [Maquinamon] cards from hand, trash, or this Digimon's digivolution cards without paying. At the end of the opponent's turn, once per turn and for each link card, it trashes the opponent's top security card and returns one opposing Digimon to the bottom of its deck.

The module uses exclusive `registerIrCard`, has full coverage, and encodes the DNA requirement, static keywords, exact name matching, DNA-only link condition, source zones, self-link scaling, once-per-turn frequency, and ordered per-link end-turn actions.

#### Evidence

| Clause | Proof | IR |
|---|---|---|
| Catalog, DNA, and coverage | Structural tests verify level, colors, cost, traits, both DNA materials, cost 0, full coverage, and exclusive registration | DNA requirement and compiled effects |
| Public DNA digivolution | Public DNA flow using legal green/black level-6 materials creates ExMaquinamon at cost 0 and stacks both materials | `dnaDigivolveRequirement` |
| Static keywords | Field observation proves Security Attack +1, Blocker, and Link +2 | Static keyword grants |
| Link clause | Public DNA flow deterministically links exact Maquinamon cards from hand and trash, leaves the ExMaquinamon-name near miss in hand, and proves the optional link action is tied to DNA. A Maquinamon linked to a DNA material is publicly observed moving to trash before the result's link effect can select it again from trash, proving the Q5945 disposal ordering. | `WhenDigivolving` `Link` |
| End-of-opponent-turn clause | Two real opponent-turn cycles with two linked cards prove each cycle trashes security before returning an opposing Digimon to deck bottom; the once-per-turn effect resets on the next cycle. | `EndOfOpponentsTurn` repeat groups and `frequency: OncePerTurn` |

Q5945 is directly regression-tested through a public DNA flow. The material's pre-existing Maquinamon link produces a `cardsMoved` event to trash during DNA assembly, before ExMaquinamon's own `When Digivolving` link action can legally select that same card from trash again. The final linked list therefore contains three Maquinamon cards, including the original instance, while the movement event proves it was not carried through the material stack. This is the correct two-step behavior; no DNA-disposal engine correction is currently indicated. Q5946 has a legal public producer in EX11-070: Mind Link places the Tamer into the target's digivolution stack (never its `linked` list). A public real-turn flow now proves Q5946 directly: EX11-070 Mind Links onto an existing EX11-073 ExMaquinamon, the Tamer is observed in the ExMaquinamon digivolution stack, and the linked list remains empty. The earlier attempted cross-turn fixture used EX11-036 as the host; its inherited EX11-070 End of All Turns effect legally plays the Tamer out of that stack at the turn boundary, so that disappearance was expected inherited behavior rather than Mind Link cleanup or DNA disposal. Q5947 is behaviorally covered across two real opponent-turn cycles: all security trashes precede deck-bottom return on each reset.

Fixtures use ordinary Digimon and main-deck cards; no Digi-Egg appears outside a legal context. No injected timing APIs, `it.fails`, or skips remain.

### EX11-074 — — Vortexdramon

Vortexdramon is a green level 7 Digimon with `[Bird Dragon]`, `[Vortex Warriors]`, and `[LIBERATOR]` traits. Its catalog clauses are:

- alternate `[Digivolve]` while you have `[Shoto Kazama]`: `[GrandGalemon]` for cost 6;
- `<Piercing>`, `<Vortex>`, and `<Blocker>`;
- `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking]` you may suspend one Digimon; if this effect suspended your Digimon, until the opponent's turn ends their Digimon effects do not affect this Digimon and it gets +6000 DP;
- `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` when any Digimon suspends, this Digimon may unsuspend, then may battle one opposing Digimon.

The local KB covers Q5948–Q5959. Q5948 permits choosing either player's Digimon for the first suspension. Q5949–Q5954 define the opponent-Digimon-effect immunity and its timing. Q5955–Q5959 define the optional direct battle and its interaction with Piercing and attacks. The reset proof uses two legal EX11-026 On Play suspension producers, explicitly declines the producer-turn unsuspend, then accepts the watcher from attacker A and attacker B on separate opponent turns; preferred target IDs keep each direct battle on the attacking Digimon.

#### Clause → public test → IR

| Clause | Public evidence | IR evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Alternate evolution gate and cost | Public digivolution of EX11-032 GrandGalemon with EX11-062 Shoto succeeds at cost 6; missing Shoto and wrong-name EX11-033 peers are rejected. | `digivolutionRequirement` uses exact GrandGalemon name, cost 6, alternate flag, and Shoto controller gate. |
| Keywords | The public evolved permanent reports Piercing, Vortex, and Blocker. | Three static keyword declarations are present. |
| Digivolving suspend and protection | A public GrandGalemon → Vortexdramon evolution selects the own ally, suspends it, raises Vortexdramon from 14,000 to 23,000 DP (the card's +6000 plus Shoto's Vortex Warriors +3000), and installs opponent-Digimon-effect restriction. | `WhenDigivolving` and `WhenAttacking` share optional any-field Digimon suspension, conditional opponent-Digimon restriction, and +6000 DP through opponent-turn end. |
| Opponent-effect boundary | The restriction is publicly installed after suspending the own ally. A production-loop attempt with opponent EX11-026 targeting Vortexdramon exposed an engine seam: the opponent Digimon Suspend still changes `isSuspended` despite the restriction, so no passing immunity claim is awarded. | Restriction is limited by `fromSourceKind: ["Digimon"]`, `byOpponentEffectsOnly`, and `lastSuspendedIsMine`. |
| All Turns once-per-turn and direct battle | A public opponent attack suspends its attacker; Vortexdramon unsuspends and directly battles/deletes that attacker while the opponent's security remains unchanged. A second public opponent turn repeats the same reaction after EX11-026 publicly suspends Vortexdramon and its first optional unsuspend is declined. | `AllTurns` is `OncePerTurn`, watches any Digimon suspension, optionally unsuspends self, then optionally uses `Battle` against one opposing Digimon. |
| Piercing boundary | A public Vortexdramon attack deletes a suspended opponent Digimon and consumes exactly one security card. | Static `Piercing` and normal attack resolution. |

#### Peer, stack, and zone evidence

Evolution tests use the legal GrandGalemon/Shoto route, a missing-Tamer boundary, and a same-level/same-color wrong-name EX11-033 peer. Public selection prefers an own ally for Q5948's first suspension. The All Turns tests use a public attack to suspend the opponent's Digimon and a two-cycle EX11-026 producer flow to verify direct battle without a security check. No Digi-Egg is used.

#### Q&A coverage

Q5948 is covered by the public own-Digimon suspension route. Q5949–Q5954 are partially covered by the public installed restriction and DP result. A production-loop EX11-026 opponent-Digimon suspend attempt reaches the target but the engine still suspends Vortexdramon, so the restriction-to-action seam is unresolved without shared-engine changes. The trace is specific: `runBoardAction` forwards the opponent seat/card to `ctx.fx.suspend`; `suspend` checks only `isSuspended` and `beSuspended`, then calls `access.suspend`. It never calls the existing shared `isRestricted` gate, whose source-kind and opponent-seat logic already handles `beAffected`. The smallest general fix is therefore to replace the `beSuspended` check in the effect-driven `suspend` primitive with `isRestricted(permanentId, "beSuspended")` (or equivalently add the same `beAffected` source-kind/opponent check there), preserving combat's direct `access.suspend` path. The affected regression coverage belongs in the shared primitive/mechanic tests: add a source-kind-qualified opponent `beAffected` restriction case beside `primitives.test.ts`'s existing hand-written mutation test and assert a public Digimon effect such as EX11-026 leaves the target unsuspended while a same-controller effect remains legal. EX11-074 can only retain the legal public characterization until that engine test and fix land. Q5955–Q5959 are covered at the direct-battle/no-security boundary plus one normal Piercing security check; nested direct-battle-during-attack permutations remain unexercised.

#### EX11-001 Koromon

- **Q5787** (2026-02-06): When multiple "when your Digimon attacks, you may digivolve that Digimon" effects trigger, I activated the 1st effect, then the digivolved Digimon's [When Digivolving] effect triggered. At such times, in what order can I activate the other "when your Digimon attacks, you may digivolve that Digimon" effects and that [When Digivolving] effect?
  - The [When Digivolving] effect activates first.
As soon as your Digimon attacks, the "when your Digimon attacks, you may digivolve that Digimon" effects trigger simultaneously.
You activate the 1st effect, digivolve a Digimon, then its [When Digivolving] effect triggers, and you therefore activate it first due to the derived trigger rule.

#### EX11-006 Flickmon

- **Q5793** (2026-02-06): What does a card with "X in its text" refer to?
  - It refers to a card that contains the specified text or icon in its name, traits, effects, inherited effects, (Rule), digivolution requirements, DNA digivolution, DigiXros requirements, burst digivolve, App Fusion, Link, or Assembly requirements.
For example, a card with [Knightmon] in its text would include cards with the name [DarkKnightmon] and cards with the text [Knightmon] in their effects.

Upon a security check, a [Security] effect will immediately activate without pending activation.
